## Checks and classification — appended 14 September 2026, after the enumeration was committed at `e9cbfc9`

**Method.** Every token was traced to committed evidence in `mvy0502/gencp-validation` (`tubitak/docs/evidence/`, `tubitak/docs/gates/`, `tubitak/configs/`, the frozen scripts, and the upstream code at the audited commit) and recomputed from it where a computation exists; a document in either repository counts as the artifact only for a definition (a registration's threshold or list) or a bibliographic year. Class (a) exact; (b) matches under the rounding named; (c) differs; (d) no artifact. Tokens that are plain counts of things in the text itself are (a). Nothing in the manuscript was changed.

### The (c) and (d) items in full

**(c) differs**

1. `02-methods.tex:199` — "roughly 3.9 % of the reported variance". Not reproducible from the printed 2.89 m and 14.5–17.3 m: (2.89/14.5)² = 4.0 %, (2.89/17.3)² = 2.8 %. The record (`paper-context-addendum.md` §19) states 3.9 % without a derivation, so the letter copied an underived figure.
2. `02-methods.tex:146` — "the 1.4–2.0 px residuals under test". Table I's fine-tuned arm means are 1.393, 1.456, 2.065 and 2.070 px; the range is 1.4–2.1 at the table's own rounding.
3. `03-results.tex:366` — "leaves each fine-tuned comparison within 0.02 px of its raw value". Per arm and seed the equal-count and raw render-minus-arm contrasts differ by up to 0.082 px (C2), 0.073 (C1), 0.062 (C5) and 0.058 (C4). Only the six-seed means per arm differ by 0.019 px or less, and that aggregation is stated nowhere; the results document contains no "0.02" at all.
4. `04-alternatives.tex:85` and `:175` — "the arm ordering is preserved in every condition cell except two". The record says something weaker: "C2 ranks first, or ties C3 within noise, in every condition cell except two". `pkgA_summary.json` records six further set-level cells marked "rank unstable within noise" (C3 ↔ C2 five times at ank30_prod and ank30_ovp; C1 ↔ pretrained twice at ank30_prod under phase correlation). None displaces C2 from first place, which is what the record's sentence protects and the letter's does not.
5. `04-alternatives.tex:169` — "censors 15.8 % of chips at its bound". The 15.8 % is 147 of 930 MI measurements pooled over four chip sets and four arms; in the Ankara set the paragraph is about, it is 17.9 % (70 of 390), and the unit is measurements, not chips.
6. (`04-alternatives.tex:175` counted with item 4.)

**(d) no artifact**

7. `01-introduction.tex:52–54` — 0 of 24 extents, median two days, worst seventeen, archive cap 100: `positioning-results.md` §E1 table only. `evidence/positioning-E1-extents.csv` lists the 24 extents but not the scene query's results.
8. `01-introduction.tex:56–59` — the five-year-old (2021) reference, the 2026 target, and +0.008 ± 0.031 px: `positioning-results.md` §E3 table only; no per-tile artifact under `evidence/E3/` (its three JSONs are the E3a/E3b displacement-recovery summaries, a different measurement).
9. `02-methods.tex:144` — "69 of 130 chips have no common points": `common-support-registration.md` table only.
10. `02-methods.tex:199` — 14.5 to 17.3 m: the upstream paper's reported sigma, present only in `paper-context-addendum.md` §19; the letter cites nothing at that number.
11. `03-results.tex:336` — 54.3 % and 22.1 %: the cross-platform attenuation rests on the seed-42 Kaggle loss logs, which are not committed; the figures exist only in `warmup-deconfound-results.md` §4a.
12. `04-alternatives.tex:199` and `06-data-availability.tex:31` — 86 %: the lost Phase D decomposition, disclosed as lost.
13. `05-discussion.tex:79` — torchmetrics 0.11.0: the upstream paper's statement; external by nature.

**(b) with a reader-arithmetic mismatch (the III-J class)**

14. `03-results.tex:100` — "differ by 0.005 px": the unrounded difference of the six-seed means is 0.0057, which rounds to 0.006; 0.005 is what the table's rounded entries give.
15. `03-results.tex:290` — "to within 1.5 % there (0.986)": 1 − 0.9856 = 1.44 %; a reader gets 1.4 % from the printed value. True only as a bound.
16. `04-alternatives.tex:75` and `:142` — "6.3 standard errors": 0.399/0.0635 = 6.28; from the printed 0.399/0.064 a reader gets 6.2.
17. `04-alternatives.tex:69` — "a factor of 3.6 ... in every seed": per seed the factor runs 3.48–3.62; 3.6 is the ratio of six-seed means.
18. `03-results.tex:365` — "every one at six standard errors or more": the smallest |t| is 6.01 (L1-only, seed 47). True, at the edge of the rounding.

## Part B — the second end-to-end read, at `09a7aa1` (11 pages)

Read cold, front to back, with the repository open. Locations are section and, where useful, source line.

### B.1 (a) Cross-references that do not say what the pointer claims

1. **IV-C and Table II: "two registrations that share no matcher, no chip set and no scoring code."** Both registrations were scored on the same four chip sets (ank130, eu150, ank30_prod, ank30_ovp; `B3_scores.csv` and `pkgA_scores.csv` carry identical `set` values) and on the same seed-42 generated images. "No matcher" is true; "no chip set" is false.
2. **IV-A "Section III's honest-limit paragraph"** — every other pointer names the subsection; this is III-K.
3. **II-D "Fig. 4"** — the first reference to Fig. 4 falls in Section II, before Fig. 2 (III-B) and Fig. 3 (III-E) are first referenced. IEEE numbers by first reference; the order of first references is now 1, 4, 2, 3. (Before P9 it was 2, 1 — the defect is older than the renumbering, but the renumbering widened it.)
4. **DA "(Section II-A and III-C)"** for the two-seed block and the hardware gate — III-C mentions the two-seed block's interaction only; the reader looking for the gate finds it in II-A alone. Minor.

### B.1 (b) Abstract against the sections

5. "The mechanism is plausibility pressure, not the adversarial term." III-K and V-C say invention is a necessary condition, not a complete explanation, and III-F says the LPIPS-only result "widens the claim". The abstract sentence states a mechanism; the body states which pressure and disclaims completeness. Read strictly, the abstract speaks one notch more strongly than III-K, though the preceding abstract sentence carries the "necessary condition" qualifier.
6. Everything else in the abstract is matched by a body statement with the same strength: 1/64 and "under L1 as well" (III-B), the LPIPS-only penalty (III-D), lowest/highest edge density (III-F), the render "where it matches ... including at equal counts" (III-L), point yield and coverage (III-L), the design rule (V-A).

### B.1 (c) Undefined or inconsistently used terms

7. **"BT.601 gray"** — first use is II-D (source line 124), undefined there; the definition sits in the Fig. 4 caption (III-F). See B.3: this is a regression of P4 ruling 5.1, introduced by the P6 band-handling sentence.
8. **"production path"** — first use is II-D ("the production-path figure of Section V-A"), defined only in V-A. Same regression, same P6 sentence.
9. **Arm names** — "L1-only" (34 uses), "L1 only" (Table I, Fig. 1), "L1 alone" (II-B, II-D); "LPIPS-only"/"LPIPS only"/"LPIPS alone" likewise; "adversarial + L1" with spaces (6) and "adversarial+L1" without (5). Fig. 1 fixes one spelling per cell; the text does not follow it.
10. **"the earlier arms"** (II-D, the tolerance sentence) — never identified; the record's Registration A arms are pretrained, C1, C2 and C3, and C3 does not exist in the letter.
11. **"main stage"** (III-J) — used before the reader is told that "main" epochs are the ones after the warm-up (II-A says "eighteen main epochs" without the word "stage"). Minor.

### B.1 (d) The sixteen binding sentences

| # | status | where |
|---|---|---|
| 1 registration statement verbatim | held | II-G |
| 2 "to our knowledge", never "first" | **strained**: Section I's first paragraph says the trivial baseline "had never been scored", an absolute novelty claim with no "to our knowledge" | I |
| 3 C4 − C1 never "null" | held; III-A says the adversarial arms "do not order" and differ by 0.005 px | III-A |
| 4 point-count argument names the Ankara-130 panel | held | III-G |
| 5 mediation labels | not applicable | — |
| 6 discriminator not published, seeded random start | held | II-C, V-C |
| 7 "repository's executable definition" | held | II-B |
| 8 Arar distinction | held | I |
| 9 Liu/Zhang/Xiong distinction | held | I |
| 10 Blau and Michaeli not contradicted | held | I, V-B |
| 11 MI margin a lower bound, censoring stated | held (the 15.8 % unit is item 5 of the (c) list) | IV-C |
| 12 descriptor deltas on intersections, n labelled | held | IV-C, Table II |
| 13 1/256 finding with the variance qualifier in the same paragraph, "text-versus-data inconsistency, not as a code bug" | held as applicable since P4 restored the finding; the 3.9 % itself is (c) item 1 | II-F |
| 14 invention necessary, not complete | held | III-K, V-C |
| 15 interaction disclosure, five elements | held | III-C |
| 16 within-platform and cross-platform attenuation both reported | held | III-J |

### B.1 (e) Claims drifted beyond the evidence

12. **IV-C / Table II "the arm ordering is preserved in every condition cell except two"** — (c) item 4 above: the record protects C2's first place, the letter claims full-ordering preservation.
13. **IV-C "share no chip set"** — item 1 above.
14. **III-F "Those two chips ... are excluded by rule from the comparison on the informative mask"** — true, but the informative-mask test excludes three chips (`informative_mask.json` skips ank_3_34, ank_6_38 and ank_8_31; n = 127), and neither III-H, IV-A nor Table II ever states n = 127. A denominator the letter's own standard requires.
15. **II-A "Training used ... 20 epochs per arm, under the generator's own linear schedule — ten epochs at the base learning rate followed by ten decaying to zero."** True of the non-adversarial arms only; the next paragraph gives the adversarial arms 2 + 8 + 10. Not false, but the first sentence reads as a statement about all arms.
16. **III-L "every one at six standard errors or more"** — holds at 6.01; a claim resting on the second decimal.

### B.2 New surfaces

**Fig. 1 (design) and its caption.** The panel shows what the caption says: names, repository codes, the fifth arm outside the grid with its note, three arrows from subtrahend to minuend. Two defects: (i) the caption's "three seed-level contrasts of Section III-B" — the secondary is III-D (your item); (ii) the caption does not say that the two horizontal arrows point in opposite directions because each contrast is written in the direction it was registered, which is the one thing a reader will ask.

**Fig. 2 (sign replication) and its caption.** The panels show six labelled points, a bar, a band and a zero line each, and the caption describes those. Defects: (i) "of this section" — III-D again (your item); (ii) three independent y-scales under one shared y-label with no caption clause (your item; the secondary panel's range is 0.12 px against 0.65 and 0.8); (iii) the zero line, below.

**The equations against the prose.** No divergence found between (1a)/(1b) and the II-D and III-B prose, or between (2) and II-E; both were derived from the code and the prose was checked against the same code. Repetition: II-E's sentence after (2) restates the equation in words, which is a gloss and earns its place; the Fig. 3 caption restates (1a) in words ("per seed, the chip mean over 130 chips of the adversarial arm's per-chip median residual minus its non-adversarial counterpart's") instead of citing it; III-H and IV-A state the informative-mask result in near-identical sentences ("because blur suppresses edges uniformly while restraint suppresses them conditionally" appears in both, with the same 0.986/0.277/3.6), and Table II row 1 states it a third time. One of III-H and IV-A is simple repetition.

**Section III-L, read cold.** The argument is complete and every number states its denominator except the one in (c) item 3. Wording: "its per-chip median residual is 0.583 px" reads as a per-chip value; it is the median over chips of per-chip medians. "with the Table I arms' matcher and settings unchanged" is correct and is the sentence a reviewer needs. The selection-objection paragraph is the strongest paragraph in the section. One sentence overreaches slightly: "two independent truncations moving the same way is evidence against a selection-artefact account" — the qualifier "to the extent those two numbers support it" saves it, barely.

**The renumbering, beyond the mechanical check.** Two sentences now read oddly: II-D sends the reader to Fig. 4 from page 3 (item 3 above), and the DA statement's "the 96 epoch checkpoints behind Fig. 3" is fine. Nothing else changed meaning.

### B.3 Regression check against the P3 findings and the P4 rulings

Every P4 ruling was checked in the current source. Holding: 1.1 (III-G names the panel), 1.2 (the 1/256 finding is in II-F with its qualifier), 1.3 (both attenuation figures with metric names), 2.1 (IV-D narrowed and pointing at V-A), 2.2 (Table II caption: row 1 six-seed, rows 2–3 seed 42), 2.3 (V-C names the one row), 2.4 (II-C and V-C carry "single-seed, chip-level"), 2.5 (III-I: study record, SHA, date), 2.6 ("Cohen, Kligvasser et al." at both sites), 2.7 (DA excludes the 24 cells explicitly), 3.1 (III-B states the L1-family replication), 3.2 (abstract carries the warm-up exception), 3.4 (abstract at III-K's strength), 4.1 (IV-A conditional), 4.2 (III-E states the L1 non-monotonicity), 4.3 (V-A states its path), 4.4 (II-E "to our knowledge" with the BIGPrior citation), 5.2 (one name; the lost package's sweep distinguished), 5.3 (input-silent defined once, IV-A back-references).

3.3 holds by removal: the "yields more, but worse, matches" clause no longer exists (P7's abstract trim removed it).

**Not holding: 5.1 for two of its seven terms.** At P4 (6ba9786) the first uses of "BT.601 gray" and "production path" were in the Fig. 4 caption and V-A, where they are defined. P6's band-handling sentence in II-D (source line 124–125) now uses both before their definitions. The other five terms (informative mask, training-stability rule, hardware gate, match score, chip) still define on first use.

P3 findings 1–25 are all closed in the current text except finding 24's descendant (item 5 above, the abstract's "The mechanism is") and the two term regressions.

### B.4 Your three items

1. **Fig. 1 / Fig. 2 captions, "Section III-B".** Correct. The secondary contrast is reported in III-D; the primary and the L1 family are in III-B.
2. **Fig. 2's three scales.** Correct. The panels are autoscaled per contrast (the script pads each to its own range), the y-label is shared, and no caption clause says so; the secondary panel's spread is roughly a tenth of the primary's.
3. **The zero line in the secondary panel.** A zero line is drawn inside the frame in all three panels (the script sets the lower limit 18 % of the range below zero). In the primary panel it sits near the top and is unmistakable. In the L1-family and secondary panels it sits 15 % above the bottom of the plotting area, and because the bottom spine is hidden it reads as the axis baseline rather than as a reference line. So you are right that it cannot be told from the PDF, and the defect is in both positive-contrast panels, not only the secondary.
